# Prompt Optimization Plan: Medical LLM Pipeline

## Context

The report at `instructions/prompt_improvements/Optimizing Medical LLM Prompts.md` identifies systemic inefficiencies in our Jinja2 prompt templates and model configuration. Cross-referenced with our own research on MedGemma prompting (MEDIQA-OE 2025 findings), Gemini structured output best practices, and LangGraph patterns, this plan addresses concrete issues in our 6 prompt templates, 3 inline prompts, and model configuration to reduce token waste, eliminate instruction contradictions, and improve grounding accuracy.

The changes will be validated against `tests/e2e/test_snippets.json` golden examples (7 extraction + 9 grounding snippets) to ensure no accuracy regressions.

---

## Branch

Create `feature/prompt-optimizations` from `main`.

---

## Changes Overview

| # | File | Issue | Change |
|---|------|-------|--------|
| 1 | `grounding_system.jinja2` | Overlapping confidence bands; duplicated search strategy | Tighten scoring to mutually exclusive bands; remove search strategy (duplicates reasoning template) |
| 2 | `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` | Instruction sandwich (confidence scale restated); key instruction not last | Remove redundant confidence scale; move decisive instruction to final line |
| 3 | `grounding_reasoning.jinja2` | Latent knowledge waste (abbreviation dictionary); instruction sandwich | Remove abbreviation examples MedGemma already knows; consolidate footer |
| 4 | `entity_decompose.jinja2` | Category duplication (4 Lab_Value examples); verbose "Other" avoidance | Prune to 1 example per entity type; consolidate "Other" rule to 1 line |
| 5 | `system.jinja2` | Markdown formatting vulnerable to instruction drift in long context; verbose composite splitting rules | Convert to XML-tagged sections for Gemini; consolidate overlapping split rules |
| 6 | `user.jinja2` | Redundant instructions already in system prompt | Slim down to essentials |
| 7 | `field_mapper.py` inline prompt | Very verbose (~50 lines); mixes schema description with anti-patterns | Restructure with XML sections; cut anti-pattern block by 60% |
| 8 | `structure_builder.py` inline prompt | Minor: no structural issues | Light cleanup only |
| 9 | `ordinal_resolver.py` inline prompt | Minor: "NOT ordinal" section is verbose | Trim negative examples |
| 10 | `model_garden.py` | `### Instruction:` prefix not in Gemma training template; `max_output_tokens=8192` wasteful | Remove prefix; reduce default to 512 |
| 11 | `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` | Static 200-char context slice for all entity types | Dynamic context: skip context for unambiguous demographics |

---

## Detailed Changes

### 1. `grounding_system.jinja2`
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/grounding_system.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Confidence bands overlap: "0.9-1.0 exact" then later "at least 0.8 if clearly same concept"
- Lines 30-36 contain a "Search Strategy" section that duplicates `grounding_reasoning.jinja2` lines 35-41
- 36 lines total, ~250 tokens per MedGemma call

**Changes:**
- Remove the "Search Strategy" section entirely (it's only relevant during retry, which uses `grounding_reasoning.jinja2`)
- Tighten confidence scoring to 4 mutually exclusive bands with no overlap
- Result: ~20 lines, ~150 tokens (40% reduction per call)

### 2. `grounding_evaluate.jinja2`
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/grounding_evaluate.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Lines 23-24 restate the confidence scale already defined in `grounding_system.jinja2`
- The IMPORTANT context-validation rule (line 29) duplicates rule 7-8 in `grounding_system.jinja2`
- Key instruction ("Review the candidates and select the best match") is at line 20, not at the end

**Changes:**
- Remove the inline confidence scale (lines 23-24) - the system prompt already defines this
- Remove the duplicate context-validation paragraph (already in system prompt rules 7-8)
- Move the decisive action instruction ("Select the best match...") to be the last line before model generation
- Add dynamic context slicing: `{% if entity_type != "Demographic" %}` around criterion context injection

### 3. `grounding_reasoning.jinja2`
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/grounding_reasoning.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Lines 36-38 list common abbreviation expansions (HTN, MI, DM, CKD) - MedGemma 4B-IT's medical pretraining already knows these
- Lines 43-48 form an instruction sandwich: "Respond with a brief clinical analysis. Address all three questions." restates the intro
- Total: ~48 lines, ~350 tokens per retry call

**Changes:**
- Remove the abbreviation dictionary from Q3's simplification strategies (keep the strategy descriptions, just remove the specific examples that MedGemma already knows)
- Remove the instruction sandwich footer (lines 43-48)
- Strengthen the IMPORTANT directive for previous query divergence (keep it - this is a legitimate unique instruction)
- Result: ~35 lines, ~250 tokens (30% reduction)

### 4. `entity_decompose.jinja2`
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/entity_decompose.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Rules section has 4 Lab_Value examples (eGFR, BMI, Potassium, LVEF) - only 1 needed per the report's "one high-variance example per category" principle
- Rules 7-8 both address "Other" avoidance in different ways (negative constraint bloat)
- 53 lines total, called once per criterion (~40 criteria per protocol = high volume)

**Changes:**
- Prune examples to exactly 1 per entity type (6 examples for 6 types):
  - Lab_Value: keep "eGFR" (most complex - abbreviation + unit)
  - Condition: keep "myocardial infarction"
  - Medication: keep "calcium channel blockers"
  - Demographic: keep "age"
  - Procedure: keep "coronary artery bypass graft"
  - Other: remove (no example needed for last-resort category)
- Consolidate rules 7+8 into a single positive statement: "Classify clinical scoring systems as Lab_Value"
- Keep compound concept rules and medical normalization rules intact (these are domain-critical, not redundant)
- Result: ~45 lines (save 8 lines x ~40 calls = ~320 tokens saved per protocol)

### 5. `system.jinja2` (extraction)
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/system.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Uses Markdown formatting (## headers, **bold**, numbered lists) - research shows XML tags improve instruction adherence in Gemini for long-context extraction
- Composite criteria splitting section has overlapping DO/DON'T examples
- 232 lines total - this is the longest prompt and is used for the most expensive call (PDF extraction)

**Changes:**
- Wrap major sections in XML tags (`<role>`, `<rules>`, `<examples>`, `<output_format>`) while keeping content in Markdown within tags (hybrid approach preserves readability)
- Consolidate composite splitting: merge the "Split these" and "Do NOT split these" into a single decision rule with the positive framing recommended by the report
- Reduce assertion status examples: currently 3 examples each for 5 statuses = 15 examples. Reduce to 2 each = 10 examples (5 lines saved x high token cost)
- Keep all few-shot extraction examples intact (these are critical for structured output accuracy with Gemini)

### 6. `user.jinja2` (extraction)
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/user.jinja2`

**Current issues:**
- Lines 6-20 repeat instructions already given in system prompt (negation markers, conditional statements, time-based requirements)
- This repetition is the "instruction sandwich" anti-pattern

**Changes:**
- Remove the bullet list of "Pay special attention to" items (already in system prompt)
- Keep only: protocol title, the core instruction ("Extract all inclusion and exclusion criteria"), and the scope statement
- Result: ~5 lines (from 20)

### 7. `field_mapper.py` inline prompt
**Path:** `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py`

**Current issues:**
- 50-line inline prompt string with extensive anti-pattern examples
- Mixes role, instructions, schema description, and anti-patterns in a single block

**Changes:**
- Restructure into XML-tagged sections: `<role>`, `<entity_context>`, `<rules>`, `<value_types>`
- Cut the anti-pattern block from 6 examples to 2 (keep the most common failure modes: boolean normalization and temporal format)
- Remove the "CRITICAL" repetitions (boolean normalization is stated twice currently)
- The schema is already enforced by Pydantic `FieldMappingResponse` - don't re-describe field types in prose

### 8. `model_garden.py` configuration
**Path:** `libs/inference/src/inference/model_garden.py`

**Current issues:**
- `_build_gemma_prompt()` adds `### Instruction:\n` prefix to user turns - this is NOT part of the Gemma training template and wastes tokens
- `max_output_tokens=8192` default is 16x more than needed for grounding decisions (~150-200 tokens)

**Changes:**
- Remove the `### Instruction:\n` prefix from `_build_gemma_prompt()` (line 65)
- Reduce `ModelGardenChatModel.max_output_tokens` default from 8192 to 512
- Reduce `LocalMedGemmaChatModel.max_output_tokens` default from 4096 to 512

---

## Validation Strategy

### Manual evaluation against test_snippets.json

Write a standalone evaluation script `scripts/evaluate_prompt_changes.py` that:

1. **Extraction evaluation**: Feeds each of the 7 `extraction_test_snippets` through `entity_decompose.jinja2` via Gemini and verifies:
   - Correct entity extraction (entity names match golden examples)
   - Correct entity type classification
   - No spurious entities extracted from "neither" snippets

2. **Grounding evaluation**: Feeds each of the 9 `grounding_test_snippets` through the grounding prompt chain (entity decompose -> grounding evaluate) and verifies:
   - Entity names match golden `entity_name` values (fuzzy match)
   - Terminology codes match golden `code` values (exact match)
   - Relations match golden `relation` values (after normalization)
   - Confidence scores are >= 0.5 for all golden entities

3. **Token counting**: Measures and reports total input/output tokens for old vs new prompts using `tiktoken` approximate counting.

4. **Regression check**: Compares new prompt outputs against existing `test_grounding_snippets.py` assertions to ensure all 9 named tests still pass.

### Existing test suite
Run `uv run pytest tests/e2e/test_grounding_snippets.py -v` to verify structural golden assertions still hold.

## Parallelization Plan

### Phase 1: Branch creation + research (me)
- Create `feature/prompt-optimizations` branch
- Coordinate subagent work

### Phase 2: Parallel implementation (6 Sonnet subagents)

**Agent A**: Optimize `grounding_system.jinja2` + `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` + `grounding_reasoning.jinja2` (changes 1-3)
- These three templates are tightly coupled (system/evaluate/reasoning grounding chain)
- Must be done together to avoid cross-reference breakage

**Agent B**: Optimize `entity_decompose.jinja2` (change 4)
- Self-contained template, no cross-dependencies

**Agent C**: Optimize `system.jinja2` + `user.jinja2` (changes 5-6)
- Extraction prompt pair, tightly coupled

**Agent D**: Optimize `field_mapper.py` + `structure_builder.py` + `ordinal_resolver.py` inline prompts (changes 7-9)
- All follow the same Gemini structured output pattern

**Agent E**: Fix `model_garden.py` configuration (change 10)
- Self-contained model config changes

**Agent F**: Write `scripts/evaluate_prompt_changes.py` evaluation script (validation)
- Reads test_snippets.json, renders old/new prompts, compares token counts

### Phase 3: Validation (1 Sonnet subagent)
- Run the evaluation script against test_snippets.json
- Run `uv run pytest tests/e2e/test_grounding_snippets.py -v`
- Report results and any regressions

## Expected Impact

- **Token reduction**: ~30-40% fewer input tokens per grounding call; ~15% fewer for extraction
- **Accuracy**: No regression expected - changes remove redundancy, not information
- **Latency**: Reduced token count directly reduces inference latency
- **Cost**: Proportional to token reduction across all API calls

     1→You are a medical terminology expert. Your task is to select the best terminology code for a medical entity extracted from a clinical trial eligibility criterion.
     2→
     3→You will be given:
     4→- The entity text and type
     5→- The criterion context it came from
     6→- A list of terminology candidates from medical APIs (UMLS, SNOMED, RxNorm, etc.)
     7→
     8→Your job: Select the single best match or determine no match exists.
     9→
    10→## Confidence Scoring Guidelines
    11→
    12→- **0.9-1.0**: Exact match — entity text maps directly to the concept's preferred term or is a recognized synonym
    13→- **0.7-0.8**: Close synonym — entity is a common medical synonym (e.g., "heart attack" → "myocardial infarction")
    14→- **0.5-0.6**: Partial/broader match — concept is related but broader or narrower than the entity
    15→- **0.0**: No valid match or entity is not a meaningful medical concept
    16→
    17→## Rules
    18→
    19→1. Prefer exact or near-exact concept matches over broad categories
    20→2. For medications, prefer RxNorm over UMLS when both are available
    21→3. For conditions and procedures, UMLS or SNOMED is appropriate
    22→4. If the entity is a number, unit, or procedural language (not a medical concept), set confidence=0.0
    23→5. Always provide reasoning for your selection
    24→6. Output your analysis in plain text — the output will be structured by a downstream system
    25→7. Text overlap is NOT sufficient for a match. The candidate must represent the same CLINICAL MEANING as the entity in the criterion context. Candidates from the wrong clinical domain (e.g., medical devices or supplies when the entity is a clinical procedure or condition) must receive confidence 0.0 regardless of word overlap.
    26→8. Use the criterion context to validate candidate relevance. Candidates that contradict the criterion's demographic, anatomical, or clinical context must receive confidence 0.0.
    27→
    28→## Search Strategy (for retry attempts)
    29→
    30→If your first terminology search yields 0 results:
    31→- DO NOT retry the exact same phrase or minor word-order variations.
    32→- Simplify to the core medical noun: "Left ventricular hypertrophy" → "ventricular hypertrophy" → "hypertrophy".
    33→- Drop anatomical qualifiers first, then adjectives: "bilateral chronic kidney disease" → "chronic kidney disease" → "kidney disease".
    34→- Expand abbreviations: "HTN" → "hypertension", "MI" → "myocardial infarction", "DM" → "diabetes mellitus", "CKD" → "chronic kidney disease", "COPD" → "chronic obstructive pulmonary disease".
    35→- Try the generic drug class if a specific brand name fails: "Keytruda" → "pembrolizumab" → "anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody".
    36→- Each retry MUST use a substantially different query from all previous attempts.
    37→

```
     1→You are a clinical NLP entity extractor. Given a clinical trial eligibility criterion, extract ONLY the discrete medical concepts that should be grounded to terminology codes.
     2→
     3→## Rules
     4→1. Extract the medical ENTITY (not the full sentence). Examples:
     5→   - "eGFR >= 30 mL/min/1.73m2" -> text: "eGFR", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
     6→   - "No history of myocardial infarction" -> text: "myocardial infarction", entity_type: "Condition"
     7→   - "Currently taking calcium channel blockers" -> text: "calcium channel blockers", entity_type: "Medication"
     8→   - "Age 20 to 60 years" -> text: "age", entity_type: "Demographic"
     9→   - "BMI > 30 kg/m2" -> text: "body mass index", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    10→   - "Coronary artery bypass graft within 3 months" -> text: "coronary artery bypass graft", entity_type: "Procedure"
    11→   - "Potassium >= 5.0 mmol/L" -> text: "potassium", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    12→   - "LVEF <= 40%" -> text: "left ventricular ejection fraction", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    13→
    14→2. One criterion may yield 0, 1, or multiple entities.
    15→3. Skip non-groundable concepts: consent, willingness, investigator discretion, geographic location, legal capacity.
    16→4. Entity types: Condition | Medication | Lab_Value | Procedure | Demographic | Other
    17→5. The `value` field is NOT used by this tool. Only extract `text` (the medical concept) and `entity_type`.
    18→6. Extract entities even from indication or context-framing criteria. The sentence structure does not matter — extract every medical concept regardless of how it is phrased.
    19→7. Any clinical scoring system, classification scale, physical status grade, or ordinal assessment is Lab_Value — never "Other". This includes ALL scales you know of, whether common or rare.
    20→8. Use "Other" ONLY as a last resort for truly unclassifiable entities. Always prefer a specific type. If in doubt between "Other" and a specific type, choose the specific type.
    21→
    22→## Medical Normalization Rules
    23→- Always normalize entity text to the standard medical NOUN form used in terminology systems (UMLS, SNOMED, ICD-10), not adjective/adverb forms from the criterion text. Adjective forms produce poor terminology search results:
    24→  - "immunocompromised" → "immunodeficiency" (Condition)
    25→  - "hypertensive" → "hypertension" (Condition)
    26→  - "febrile" → "fever" (Condition)
    27→- When a criterion describes a state resulting from a medical intervention, extract the INTERVENTION as a Procedure — not the resulting state as a Condition. In clinical trial eligibility language, these criteria require proof of a past procedure:
    28→  - "immunized against hepatitis B" → "hepatitis B vaccination" (Procedure)
    29→  - "must have undergone appendectomy" → "appendectomy" (Procedure)
    30→  - Prefer "-tion"/"-ectomy"/"-plasty" procedure nouns over "-ity"/"-ness" state nouns, as procedure nouns match terminology systems better and avoid ambiguity.
    31→- Do NOT embed demographic qualifiers (male/female/age) into clinical entity text — those belong as separate Demographic entities. Mixing demographics into clinical terms confuses terminology search:
    32→  - "Male patients with renal failure" → "renal failure" (Condition) AND "male" (Demographic)
    33→  - "Elderly subjects with osteoporosis" → "osteoporosis" (Condition) AND "age" (Demographic)
    34→
    35→## Compound Concept Rules — DO NOT Over-Decompose
    36→- Do NOT separate anatomical modifiers, severities, laterality, or procedure types from their parent concept. These are a single medical entity:
    37→  - "tricompartmental knee replacement" → ONE entity (not "knee replacement" + "tricompartmental")
    38→  - "spinal muscular atrophy" → ONE entity
    39→  - "non-small cell lung cancer" → ONE entity
    40→  - "left ventricular hypertrophy" → ONE entity
    41→  - "chronic obstructive pulmonary disease" → ONE entity
    42→  - "bilateral total hip arthroplasty" → ONE entity
    43→- DO separate distinct comorbidities or co-occurring conditions into individual entities:
    44→  - "Type 2 diabetes with nephropathy" → TWO entities: "type 2 diabetes" AND "nephropathy"
    45→  - "Hypertension and heart failure" → TWO entities: "hypertension" AND "heart failure"
    46→  - "Asthma or COPD" → TWO entities: "asthma" AND "chronic obstructive pulmonary disease"
    47→
    48→## Criterion
    49→{% if category %}Category hint: {{ category }}{% endif %}
    50→Text: "{{ criterion_text }}"
    51→
    52→Return a JSON object with key "entities" containing an array of {"text": string, "entity_type": string} objects.
    53→If no groundable entities exist, return {"entities": []}.
    54→
```

     1→You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Your task is to extract all inclusion and exclusion criteria from the provided clinical trial protocol document.
     2→
     3→## Instructions
     4→
     5→1. Identify ALL eligibility criteria — both inclusion and exclusion
     6→2. Extract each criterion as a separate item with its complete original text
     7→3. Classify each criterion as "inclusion" or "exclusion"
     8→4. Assign a category: medical_history, lab_values, medications, procedures, or other (NOT demographics — demographic criteria are not grounded to terminology)
     9→5. Detect temporal constraints (durations, time windows, reference points)
    10→6. Extract numeric thresholds (lab values, age ranges, dosages) — see examples below
    11→7. Identify conditional dependencies between criteria — see examples below
    12→8. Determine the assertion status for each criterion (see below)
    13→9. Assign a confidence score (0.0-1.0) based on extraction certainty
    14→10. Report the 1-based page number where each criterion appears in the PDF (page_number field)
    15→11. Do NOT extract consent or participation statements (e.g., "An informed written consent will be taken from all participants"). These are procedural requirements, not medical eligibility criteria.
    16→
    17→## Composite Criteria Splitting
    18→
    19→**CRITICAL: Split independent composite criteria into separate entries.**
    20→
    21→If a criterion contains INDEPENDENT conditions joined by AND, OR, or similar connectors where each condition stands alone as a distinct eligibility requirement, split them into separate criterion entries.
    22→
    23→**Split these (independent conditions — each is a distinct requirement):**
    24→- "Age 18-65 AND BMI <30" → TWO criteria: (1) "Age 18-65", (2) "BMI <30"
    25→- "Systolic BP >140 mmHg OR diastolic BP >90 mmHg" → TWO criteria
    26→- "Hemoglobin <10 g/dL AND platelet count <100,000/μL" → TWO criteria
    27→
    28→**Do NOT split these (dependent conditions — one implies or requires the other):**
    29→- "If female of childbearing potential, must have negative pregnancy test" → ONE criterion (pregnancy test depends on sex)
    30→- "Patients with diabetes who have HbA1c >9%" → ONE criterion (HbA1c condition depends on diabetes)
    31→- "Known allergy to aspirin or NSAIDs" → ONE criterion (aspirin and NSAIDs are the same category of medication)
    32→- "No history of myocardial infarction or stroke" → ONE criterion (both are cardiovascular exclusions)
    33→
    34→**Rule:** Ask "Can each condition be evaluated independently for any patient?" If YES → split. If one condition only makes sense in context of the other → keep together.
    35→
    36→## AutoCriteria Decomposition Guidance
    37→
    38→For each criterion, structure the extracted fields to capture the AutoCriteria components:
    39→- **Entity**: The medical concept (e.g., "HbA1c", "BMI", "platelet count")
    40→- **Operator**: The comparison relationship (e.g., "<", ">=", "range", "no history of")
    41→- **Value**: The threshold value (captured in numeric_thresholds)
    42→- **Unit**: The measurement unit (e.g., "g/dL", "kg/m2", "years")
    43→- **Time**: The temporal context (captured in temporal_constraint)
    44→
    45→This decomposition supports downstream UMLS grounding of the Entity component.
    46→
    47→## Numeric Thresholds and Conditions Examples
    48→
    49→Below are examples showing how to extract numeric_thresholds and conditions. Pay special attention to: age ranges use comparator "range" with upper_value, single-bound thresholds use ">=", "<=", ">", "<", or "==". For conditions: output only short phrases taken from the protocol (e.g. "if female of childbearing potential"); one phrase per conditional dependency. Do not repeat the same phrase, and do not output instruction or placeholder text like "the criterion text provided above".
    50→
    51→<EXAMPLE>
    52→<INPUT>
    53→Male or female, 40 years to 85 years old
    54→</INPUT>
    55→<OUTPUT>
    56→{
    57→  "text": "Male or female, 40 years to 85 years old",
    58→  "criteria_type": "inclusion",
    59→  "category": "other",
    60→  "numeric_thresholds": [
    61→    {
    62→      "value": 40.0,
    63→      "unit": "years",
    64→      "comparator": "range",
    65→      "upper_value": 85.0
    66→    }
    67→  ],
    68→  "temporal_constraint": null,
    69→  "conditions": [],
    70→  "assertion_status": "PRESENT",
    71→  "confidence": 1.0
    72→}
    73→</OUTPUT>
    74→</EXAMPLE>
    75→
    76→<EXAMPLE>
    77→<INPUT>
    78→HbA1c between 6.5% and 9.5%
    79→</INPUT>
    80→<OUTPUT>
    81→{
    82→  "text": "HbA1c between 6.5% and 9.5%",
    83→  "criteria_type": "inclusion",
    84→  "category": "lab_values",
    85→  "numeric_thresholds": [
    86→    {
    87→      "value": 6.5,
    88→      "unit": "%",
    89→      "comparator": "range",
    90→      "upper_value": 9.5
    91→    }
    92→  ],
    93→  "temporal_constraint": null,
    94→  "conditions": [],
    95→  "assertion_status": "PRESENT",
    96→  "confidence": 1.0
    97→}
    98→</OUTPUT>
    99→</EXAMPLE>
   100→
   101→<EXAMPLE>
   102→<INPUT>
   103→WOMAC A pain subscale score of 1.5 or greater on a 5-point Likert scale
   104→</INPUT>
   105→<OUTPUT>
   106→{
   107→  "text": "WOMAC A pain subscale score of 1.5 or greater on a 5-point Likert scale",
   108→  "criteria_type": "inclusion",
   109→  "category": "lab_values",
   110→  "numeric_thresholds": [
   111→    {
   112→      "value": 1.5,
   113→      "unit": "WOMAC",
   114→      "comparator": ">=",
   115→      "upper_value": null
   116→    }
   117→  ],
   118→  "temporal_constraint": null,
   119→  "conditions": [],
   120→  "assertion_status": "PRESENT",
   121→  "confidence": 0.95
   122→}
   123→</OUTPUT>
   124→</EXAMPLE>
   125→
   126→<EXAMPLE>
   127→<INPUT>
   128→For patients with diabetes, HbA1c must be less than 8%
   129→</INPUT>
   130→<OUTPUT>
   131→{
   132→  "text": "For patients with diabetes, HbA1c must be less than 8%",
   133→  "criteria_type": "inclusion",
   134→  "category": "lab_values",
   135→  "numeric_thresholds": [
   136→    {
   137→      "value": 8.0,
   138→      "unit": "%",
   139→      "comparator": "<",
   140→      "upper_value": null
   141→    }
   142→  ],
   143→  "temporal_constraint": null,
   144→  "conditions": ["for patients with diabetes"],
   145→  "assertion_status": "CONDITIONAL",
   146→  "confidence": 0.9
   147→}
   148→</OUTPUT>
   149→</EXAMPLE>
   150→
   151→<EXAMPLE>
   152→<INPUT>
   153→If female of childbearing potential, must have negative pregnancy test within 7 days of enrollment
   154→</INPUT>
   155→<OUTPUT>
   156→{
   157→  "text": "If female of childbearing potential, must have negative pregnancy test within 7 days of enrollment",
   158→  "criteria_type": "inclusion",
   159→  "category": "other",
   160→  "numeric_thresholds": [
   161→    {
   162→      "value": 7.0,
   163→      "unit": "days",
   164→      "comparator": "<=",
   165→      "upper_value": null
   166→    }
   167→  ],
   168→  "temporal_constraint": {
   169→    "duration": "7 days",
   170→    "relation": "within",
   171→    "reference_point": "enrollment"
   172→  },
   173→  "conditions": ["if female of childbearing potential"],
   174→  "assertion_status": "CONDITIONAL",
   175→  "confidence": 0.9
   176→}
   177→</OUTPUT>
   178→</EXAMPLE>
   179→
   180→## Assertion Status Classification
   181→
   182→For each criterion, determine the assertion status:
   183→
   184→- **PRESENT**: The condition must be true/present for the patient.
   185→  - Example: "Age >= 18 years" → PRESENT
   186→  - Example: "Diagnosis of type 2 diabetes" → PRESENT
   187→  - Example: "ECOG performance status 0-1" → PRESENT
   188→
   189→- **ABSENT**: The condition must NOT be true/must be absent.
   190→  - Example: "No history of cardiac disease" → ABSENT
   191→  - Example: "Without prior organ transplant" → ABSENT
   192→  - Example: "Absence of active infections" → ABSENT
   193→  - Example: "No evidence of brain metastases" → ABSENT
   194→  - Example: "Free of autoimmune disorders" → ABSENT
   195→
   196→- **HYPOTHETICAL**: A theoretical or future condition.
   197→  - Example: "Willing to use contraception during study" → HYPOTHETICAL
   198→  - Example: "Able to comply with study visits" → HYPOTHETICAL
   199→  - Example: "Willing to provide informed consent" → HYPOTHETICAL
   200→
   201→- **HISTORICAL**: Refers to past medical history or prior treatment.
   202→  - Example: "Prior treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy" → HISTORICAL
   203→  - Example: "History of myocardial infarction within 6 months" → HISTORICAL
   204→  - Example: "Previous radiation therapy to the chest" → HISTORICAL
   205→
   206→- **CONDITIONAL**: Depends on another condition being true.
   207→  - Example: "If female of childbearing potential, must have negative pregnancy test" → CONDITIONAL
   208→  - Example: "For patients with diabetes, HbA1c < 8%" → CONDITIONAL
   209→  - Example: "In case of prior surgery, at least 4 weeks recovery" → CONDITIONAL
   210→
   211→## Key Negation Markers (indicate ABSENT)
   212→- "no history of", "without", "absence of", "not have", "free of", "no evidence of", "no prior", "must not", "has not", "never had"
   213→
   214→## Key Conditionality Markers (indicate CONDITIONAL)
   215→- "if", "in case of", "when applicable", "for patients who", "provided that", "unless"
   216→
   217→When populating conditions: each list entry must be a single short phrase from the protocol (the conditional part only). Do not repeat the same phrase; do not output meta-instructions or placeholders.
   218→
   219→## Output Format
   220→
   221→Return a structured extraction with:
   222→- A list of all criteria with their full structured fields
   223→- A brief protocol summary (1-2 sentences describing the trial's purpose)
   224→
   225→## Page Number Tracking
   226→
   227→For each criterion, include the `page_number` field:
   228→- Use 1-based page numbering (first page = 1)
   229→- Report the page where the criterion text begins
   230→- If a criterion spans multiple pages, use the first page
   231→- If you cannot determine the page number, set page_number to null
   232→
```

     1→"""Field mapping generation tool for grounded entities.
     2→
     3→Per user decision: "Generate suggested field_mappings during grounding (ground node)"
     4→Per CONTEXT.md: "Criteria should be decomposed per AutoCriteria pattern: separate
     5→Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time"
     6→Per user decision: "MedGemma and Gemini collaborate: Gemini uses MedGemma as
     7→medical expert"
     8→
     9→Uses Gemini to generate suggested field mappings for the grounded entity +
    10→criterion text. These are best-effort suggestions — reviewer can edit in UI.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import logging
    16→from typing import Any, Literal
    17→
    18→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator, model_validator
    19→
    20→from protocol_processor.schemas.grounding import EntityGroundingResult
    21→from protocol_processor.tools.gemini_utils import (
    22→    create_structured_llm,
    23→    parse_structured_output,
    24→)
    25→
    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27→
    28→# Mapping from legacy/LLM relation operators to the frontend's RelationOperator set
    29→_RELATION_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    30→    "has": "contains",
    31→    "is": "=",
    32→    "not": "not_contains",
    33→    "==": "=",
    34→    "range": "within",
    35→}
    36→
    37→
    38→def _normalize_relation(rel: str) -> str:
    39→    """Normalize a relation operator to the frontend's accepted set.
    40→
    41→    Maps legacy operators (has, is, not, ==, range) to the standard set:
    42→    =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within, not_in_last, contains, not_contains.
    43→    """
    44→    return _RELATION_MAP.get(rel, rel)
    45→
    46→
    47→class FieldMappingValue(BaseModel):
    48→    """Typed value object for field mappings with type discriminator.
    49→
    50→    Supports three value shapes:
    51→    - standard: single value + unit (e.g. HbA1c < 7%)
    52→    - range: min/max + unit (e.g. Age 18-65 years)
    53→    - temporal: duration + unit (e.g. within 6 months)
    54→    """
    55→
    56→    type: Literal["standard", "range", "temporal"] = Field(
    57→        description="Value type discriminator"
    58→    )
    59→    value: str | None = Field(
    60→        default=None, description="Value for standard type (e.g. '7')"
    61→    )
    62→    unit: str | None = Field(
    63→        default=None, description="Unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'months')"
    64→    )
    65→    min: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Minimum value for range type")
    66→    max: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Maximum value for range type")
    67→    duration: str | None = Field(
    68→        default=None, description="Duration value for temporal type"
    69→    )
    70→
    71→    @model_validator(mode="after")
    72→    def truncate_long_strings(self) -> "FieldMappingValue":
    73→        """Guard against LLM repetition loops producing absurdly long values."""
    74→        _max = 200
    75→        for attr in ("value", "unit", "min", "max", "duration"):
    76→            val = getattr(self, attr)
    77→            if isinstance(val, str) and len(val) > _max:
    78→                setattr(self, attr, val[:_max])
    79→        return self
    80→
    81→
    82→# The set of valid relation operators accepted by the frontend
    83→RelationOperator = Literal[
    84→    "=", "!=", ">", ">=", "<", "<=", "within", "not_in_last", "contains", "not_contains"
    85→]
    86→
    87→
    88→class FieldMappingItem(BaseModel):
    89→    """A single AutoCriteria field mapping decomposition.
    90→
    91→    Per the AutoCriteria pattern, each criterion is decomposed into
    92→    separate Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, and Time components.
    93→    """
    94→
    95→    entity: str = Field(description="The medical entity name (e.g. 'HbA1c')")
    96→    relation: RelationOperator = Field(
    97→        description="The logical operator/relation (e.g. '<', '>', '=', 'contains')"
    98→    )
    99→    value: FieldMappingValue = Field(
   100→        description="Typed value object with type discriminator"
   101→    )
   102→    unit: str | None = Field(
   103→        default=None,
   104→        description="Optional unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'years')",
   105→    )
   106→    value_concept_id: str | None = Field(
   107→        default=None,
   108→        description="OMOP concept ID for categorical values",
   109→    )
   110→    value_concept_system: str | None = Field(
   111→        default=None,
   112→        description="Terminology system for value_concept_id (e.g. 'SNOMED', 'OMOP')",
   113→    )
   114→
   115→    @field_validator("relation", mode="before")
   116→    @classmethod
   117→    def normalize_relation(cls, v: str) -> str:
   118→        """Normalize LLM-generated relation operators before Literal validation."""
   119→        if isinstance(v, str):
   120→            return _normalize_relation(v)
   121→        return v
   122→
   123→
   124→class FieldMappingResponse(BaseModel):
   125→    """Gemini structured output for field mappings."""
   126→
   127→    mappings: list[FieldMappingItem] = Field(
   128→        default_factory=list,
   129→        description="List of AutoCriteria field mapping decompositions",
   130→    )
   131→
   132→
   133→async def generate_field_mappings(
   134→    entity: EntityGroundingResult,
   135→    criterion_text: str,
   136→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   137→    """Generate suggested field mappings for a grounded entity.
   138→
   139→    Uses Gemini to decompose the criterion text into AutoCriteria field mappings:
   140→    Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time components for each discrete condition.
   141→
   142→    This is a best-effort suggestion — reviewer can edit in the UI. Errors
   143→    are logged and an empty list returned (not propagated as failures).
   144→
   145→    Args:
   146→        entity: Grounded EntityGroundingResult with code and preferred term.
   147→        criterion_text: Full criterion text for context.
   148→
   149→    Returns:
   150→        List of field mapping dicts with keys: entity, relation, value,
   151→        entity_code, entity_system, omop_concept_id, entity_type.
   152→        Empty list if generation fails.
   153→    """
   154→    if not criterion_text:
   155→        return []
   156→
   157→    structured_llm = create_structured_llm(FieldMappingResponse)
   158→    if structured_llm is None:
   159→        return []
   160→
   161→    try:
   162→        # Build a context-rich prompt for field mapping generation
   163→        grounded_term = entity.preferred_term or entity.entity_text
   164→        code_context = ""
   165→        if entity.selected_code and entity.selected_system:
   166→            system = entity.selected_system.upper()
   167→            code_context = f"(grounded to {system} code: {entity.selected_code})"
   168→
   169→        prompt = (
   170→            "You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Decompose the"
   171→            " following criterion into structured AutoCriteria field"
   172→            " mappings using the Entity-Relation-Value-Unit pattern.\n\n"
   173→            f"Medical entity: {grounded_term} {code_context}\n"
   174→            f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n\n"
   175→            "Instructions:\n"
   176→            "- Extract each discrete measurement, threshold, or condition"
   177→            " as a separate mapping\n"
   178→            "- entity: the specific measurement or concept"
   179→            " (e.g. 'HbA1c', 'Age', 'eGFR')\n"
   180→            "- relation: MUST be one of: =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within,"
   181→            " not_in_last, contains, not_contains\n"
   182→            "- CRITICAL — Boolean normalization for presence/absence:\n"
   183→            "  - If the criterion states a condition IS present, required,"
   184→            " or confirmed:\n"
   185→            '    relation="=", value={"type": "standard", "value": "True",'
   186→            ' "unit": null}\n'
   187→            "  - If the criterion states a condition is NOT present or"
   188→            " excluded:\n"
   189→            '    relation="!=", value={"type": "standard", "value": "True",'
   190→            ' "unit": null}\n'
   191→            '  - NEVER use "present", "absent", "confirmed", "positive",'
   192→            ' "negative" as value strings\n'
   193→            '- value: a typed object with a "type" discriminator:\n'
   194→            '  - Standard: {"type": "standard", "value": "7", "unit": "%"}\n'
   195→            '  - Range: {"type": "range", "min": "18", "max": "65",'
   196→            ' "unit": "years"}\n'
   197→            '  - Temporal: {"type": "temporal", "duration": "5",'
   198→            ' "unit": "days"}\n'
   199→            "- CRITICAL — Temporal values: 'duration' MUST be a plain number"
   200→            " (e.g. '5', '6', '30'). NEVER put prose or descriptions in"
   201→            " duration. Unit goes in 'unit' (e.g. 'days', 'months', 'years').\n"
   202→            '  Example: "within the past 5 days" → {"type": "temporal",'
   203→            ' "duration": "5", "unit": "days"}\n'
   204→            '  BAD: {"duration": "5-days-ago-to-present"} — just use'
   205→            ' {"duration": "5", "unit": "days"}\n'
   206→            "- Create one mapping per discrete condition in the criterion\n"
   207→            "- If no clear measurement exists, create one mapping with"
   208→            " relation='=',"
   209→            ' value={"type": "standard", "value": "True", "unit": null}\n'
   210→            "- ANTI-PATTERNS (do NOT produce these):\n"
   211→            "  BAD: relation='!=', value='present'"
   212→            " -> use relation='!=', value='True'\n"
   213→            "  BAD: relation='contains', value='confirmed'"
   214→            " -> use relation='=', value='True'\n"
   215→            "  BAD: relation='==', value='absent'"
   216→            " -> use relation='!=', value='True'\n"
   217→        )
   218→
   219→        from protocol_processor.tracing import llm_span
   220→
   221→        with llm_span("gemini_field_mapping") as llm:
   222→            llm.set_request(prompt)
   223→            result = await structured_llm.ainvoke(prompt)
   224→            llm.set_response(str(result))
   225→
   226→        response = parse_structured_output(result, FieldMappingResponse)
   227→
   228→        mappings = [
   229→            {
   230→                "entity": m.entity,
   231→                "relation": m.relation,
   232→                "value": m.value.model_dump(exclude_none=True),
   233→                "entity_code": entity.selected_code,
   234→                "entity_system": entity.selected_system,
   235→                "omop_concept_id": entity.omop_concept_id,
   236→                "entity_type": entity.entity_type,
   237→                "value_concept_id": m.value_concept_id,
   238→                "value_concept_system": m.value_concept_system,
   239→            }
   240→            for m in response.mappings
   241→        ]
   242→
   243→        logger.info(
   244→            "Generated %d field mapping(s) for entity '%s'",
   245→            len(mappings),
   246→            entity.entity_text[:50],
   247→        )
   248→        return mappings
   249→
   250→    except Exception as e:
   251→        logger.warning(
   252→            "Field mapping generation failed for entity '%s': %s",
   253→            entity.entity_text[:50],
   254→            e,
   255→            exc_info=True,
   256→        )
   257→        # Best-effort: return empty list on failure
   258→        return []
   259→

     1→"""Ordinal scale resolution tool using Gemini structured output.
     2→
     3→Identifies unrecognized clinical ordinal scoring systems (Child-Pugh, GCS,
     4→APACHE II, MELD, mRS, SOFA, etc.) and proposes YAML config entries for
     5→human review.
     6→
     7→Follows the detect_logic_structure() pattern from structure_builder.py:
     8→- Guard on GOOGLE_API_KEY
     9→- Late import of ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
    10→- with_structured_output() for typed LLM response
    11→- Return None on any failure (graceful degradation)
    12→"""
    13→
    14→from __future__ import annotations
    15→
    16→import logging
    17→from typing import Any
    18→
    19→from protocol_processor.schemas.ordinal import (
    20→    OrdinalResolutionResponse,
    21→    OrdinalScaleProposal,
    22→)
    23→from protocol_processor.tools.gemini_utils import (
    24→    create_structured_llm,
    25→    parse_structured_output,
    26→)
    27→
    28→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    29→
    30→
    31→async def resolve_ordinal_candidates(
    32→    candidates: list[dict[str, Any]],
    33→) -> OrdinalResolutionResponse | None:
    34→    """Send candidate entities to Gemini for ordinal scale identification.
    35→
    36→    Args:
    37→        candidates: List of dicts with keys 'entity_text', 'value_numeric',
    38→            'relation_operator' from AtomicCriterion records.
    39→
    40→    Returns:
    41→        OrdinalResolutionResponse with proposals, or None on failure/skip.
    42→    """
    43→    if not candidates:
    44→        return None
    45→
    46→    structured_llm = create_structured_llm(OrdinalResolutionResponse)
    47→    if structured_llm is None:
    48→        return None
    49→
    50→    try:
    51→        # Build indexed entity list for the prompt
    52→        entity_lines = []
    53→        for i, c in enumerate(candidates):
    54→            entity = c.get("entity_text", "?")
    55→            value = c.get("value_numeric", "?")
    56→            relation = c.get("relation_operator", "?")
    57→            entity_lines.append(
    58→                f'  [{i}] entity="{entity}" relation="{relation}" value={value}'
    59→            )
    60→        entities_text = "\n".join(entity_lines)
    61→
    62→        prompt = (
    63→            "You are a clinical terminology expert specializing in medical scoring "
    64→            "systems and ordinal scales.\n\n"
    65→            "For each entity below, determine if it is a clinical ordinal scoring "
    66→            "system (e.g. ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, Child-Pugh, GCS, APACHE II, MELD, "
    67→            "mRS, SOFA, etc.).\n\n"
    68→            "An ordinal scoring system has these characteristics:\n"
    69→            "- Uses discrete numeric grades/scores (not continuous measurements)\n"
    70→            "- Each grade has a specific clinical meaning\n"
    71→            "- The unit is conceptually {score}, not a physical unit\n"
    72→            "- Common in clinical trials for performance status, organ function, "
    73→            "disease severity\n\n"
    74→            "NOT ordinal scales:\n"
    75→            "- Lab values with physical units (mg/dL, g/L, IU/mL)\n"
    76→            "- Continuous measurements (weight, height, BMI)\n"
    77→            "- Binary yes/no conditions (HIV status, pregnancy)\n"
    78→            "- Age, duration, or time-based criteria\n\n"
    79→            f"Entities to evaluate:\n{entities_text}\n\n"
    80→            "For each entity that IS an ordinal scale, propose:\n"
    81→            "- scale_name: snake_case identifier for YAML config\n"
    82→            "- entity_aliases: alternative names clinicians might use\n"
    83→            "- loinc_code: LOINC code if known\n"
    84→            "- values: list of grades with descriptions\n"
    85→        )
    86→
    87→        from protocol_processor.tracing import llm_span
    88→
    89→        with llm_span("gemini_ordinal_resolution") as llm:
    90→            llm.set_request(prompt)
    91→            result = await structured_llm.ainvoke(prompt)
    92→            llm.set_response(str(result))
    93→
    94→        response = parse_structured_output(result, OrdinalResolutionResponse)
    95→
    96→        # Filter to confirmed ordinals with sufficient confidence
    97→        confirmed: list[OrdinalScaleProposal] = [
    98→            p for p in response.proposals if p.is_ordinal_scale and p.confidence >= 0.7
    99→        ]
   100→
   101→        return OrdinalResolutionResponse(proposals=confirmed)
   102→
   103→    except Exception as e:
   104→        logger.warning(
   105→            "Ordinal resolution failed: %s",
   106→            e,
   107→            exc_info=True,
   108→        )
   109→        return None
   110→
```

     1→"""MedGemma model loading for Vertex AI and local GPU backends.
     2→
     3→Provides two deployment paths for MedGemma (google/medgemma-4b-it):
     4→
     5→1. **Vertex AI Model Garden** (``MODEL_BACKEND=vertex``):
     6→   Uses ``ModelGardenChatModel``, a LangChain BaseChatModel wrapper that
     7→   calls a Vertex AI endpoint with Gemma chat template formatting and
     8→   exponential-backoff retry for transient errors.
     9→
    10→2. **Local GPU** (``MODEL_BACKEND=local``):
    11→   Uses ``LocalMedGemmaChatModel``, which loads the HuggingFace model
    12→   locally with optional 4-bit/8-bit quantization via bitsandbytes.
    13→   Requires an NVIDIA GPU with 8 GB+ VRAM for the default 4-bit config.
    14→
    15→Both backends expose a standard LangChain ``BaseChatModel`` interface so
    16→the rest of the pipeline (``medgemma_decider.py``, ``ground.py``) is
    17→backend-agnostic.
    18→"""
    19→
    20→from __future__ import annotations
    21→
    22→import logging
    23→import time
    24→from typing import Any, Callable
    25→
    26→import requests  # type: ignore[import-untyped]
    27→from langchain_core.callbacks.manager import CallbackManagerForLLMRun
    28→from langchain_core.language_models.chat_models import BaseChatModel
    29→from langchain_core.messages import AIMessage, BaseMessage
    30→from langchain_core.outputs import ChatGeneration, ChatResult
    31→from pydantic import Field
    32→from shared.lazy_cache import lazy_singleton
    33→from tenacity import (
    34→    retry,
    35→    retry_if_exception,
    36→    stop_after_attempt,
    37→    wait_exponential,
    38→)
    39→
    40→from inference.config import AgentConfig
    41→
    42→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _build_gemma_prompt(messages: list[Any]) -> str:
    46→    """Build Gemma chat template prompt from messages.
    47→
    48→    Args:
    49→        messages: List of LangChain messages.
    50→
    51→    Returns:
    52→        Formatted prompt string.
    53→    """
    54→    prompt_parts = []
    55→    for msg in messages:
    56→        role = "user"
    57→        if msg.type == "ai":
    58→            role = "model"
    59→
    60→        content = msg.content
    61→        if not isinstance(content, str):
    62→            content = str(content)
    63→
    64→        if role == "user":
    65→            content = f"### Instruction:\n{content}"
    66→
    67→        prompt_parts.append(f"<start_of_turn>{role}\n{content}<end_of_turn>")
    68→
    69→    return "\n".join(prompt_parts) + "\n<start_of_turn>model\n"
    70→
    71→
    72→def _strip_model_garden_artifacts(text: str, full_prompt: str) -> str:
    73→    """Strip echoed prompts and end-of-turn markers from response.
    74→
    75→    Note: This is now minimal because Gemini handles JSON structuring.
    76→    MedGemma's output goes to Gemini, so thinking tokens and other artifacts
    77→    are handled by Gemini's structured output parser.
    78→    """
    79→    # Strip echoed prompt if present
    80→    if text.startswith(full_prompt):
    81→        text = text[len(full_prompt) :].strip()
    82→
    83→    # Strip trailing end-of-turn marker
    84→    if text.endswith("<end_of_turn>"):
    85→        text = text[: -len("<end_of_turn>")].strip()
    86→
    87→    return text
    88→
    89→
    90→def _is_retryable_error(exception: BaseException) -> bool:
    91→    """Check if an exception is retryable (transient error).
    92→
    93→    Args:
    94→        exception: The exception to check.
    95→
    96→    Returns:
    97→        True if the exception is retryable, False otherwise.
    98→    """
    99→    # Retry on network/connection errors
   100→    if isinstance(
   101→        exception,
   102→        (
   103→            requests.exceptions.ConnectionError,
   104→            requests.exceptions.Timeout,
   105→            requests.exceptions.ChunkedEncodingError,
   106→        ),
   107→    ):
   108→        return True
   109→
   110→    # Retry on Google API transient server errors
   111→    try:
   112→        from google.api_core import exceptions as google_exceptions
   113→
   114→        if isinstance(
   115→            exception,
   116→            (
   117→                google_exceptions.ServiceUnavailable,
   118→                google_exceptions.InternalServerError,
   119→                google_exceptions.DeadlineExceeded,
   120→                google_exceptions.ResourceExhausted,
   121→            ),
   122→        ):
   123→            return True
   124→
   125→        # Do NOT retry on client errors
   126→        if isinstance(
   127→            exception,
   128→            (
   129→                google_exceptions.PermissionDenied,
   130→                google_exceptions.Unauthenticated,
   131→                google_exceptions.InvalidArgument,
   132→                google_exceptions.NotFound,
   133→                google_exceptions.AlreadyExists,
   134→                google_exceptions.FailedPrecondition,
   135→                google_exceptions.OutOfRange,
   136→            ),
   137→        ):
   138→            return False
   139→    except ImportError:
   140→        pass  # google-api-core not installed; skip Google-specific checks
   141→
   142→    # Do NOT retry on expired credentials — no point retrying with the same creds
   143→    try:
   144→        from google.auth.exceptions import RefreshError
   145→
   146→        if isinstance(exception, RefreshError):
   147→            return False
   148→    except ImportError:
   149→        pass  # google-auth not installed; skip RefreshError check
   150→
   151→    return False
   152→
   153→
   154→@retry(
   155→    retry=retry_if_exception(_is_retryable_error),
   156→    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
   157→    wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, min=1, max=10),
   158→    reraise=True,
   159→)
   160→def _predict_with_retry(
   161→    endpoint: Any, instances: list[dict[str, Any]], parameters: dict[str, Any]
   162→) -> Any:
   163→    """Call Vertex AI endpoint.predict with retry on transient errors.
   164→
   165→    Args:
   166→        endpoint: Vertex AI Endpoint object.
   167→        instances: List of prediction instances.
   168→        parameters: Prediction parameters.
   169→
   170→    Returns:
   171→        Prediction response from the endpoint.
   172→    """
   173→    return endpoint.predict(instances=instances, parameters=parameters)
   174→
   175→
   176→class ModelGardenChatModel(BaseChatModel):
   177→    """LangChain ChatModel wrapper for Vertex AI Model Garden endpoints.
   178→
   179→    Wraps a Vertex AI endpoint that serves MedGemma (or other Gemma-family
   180→    models) using the Gemma chat template for prompt formatting and
   181→    exponential-backoff retry for transient errors.
   182→
   183→    The Endpoint object is instantiated once in the constructor and reused
   184→    across calls to enable gRPC channel reuse and avoid per-call overhead.
   185→    """
   186→
   187→    endpoint_resource_name: str
   188→    project: str
   189→    location: str
   190→    max_output_tokens: int = Field(default=8192)
   191→    _endpoint: Any = None
   192→
   193→    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
   194→        """Initialize the Vertex AI Endpoint once for connection reuse."""
   195→        from google.cloud import aiplatform
   196→
   197→        self._endpoint = aiplatform.Endpoint(self.endpoint_resource_name)
   198→
   199→    def _generate(
   200→        self,
   201→        messages: list[BaseMessage],
   202→        stop: list[str] | None = None,
   203→        run_manager: CallbackManagerForLLMRun | None = None,
   204→        **kwargs: Any,
   205→    ) -> ChatResult:
   206→        """Generate a chat response from the Model Garden endpoint.
   207→
   208→        Args:
   209→            messages: List of LangChain messages.
   210→            stop: Optional stop sequences (unused by Model Garden).
   211→            run_manager: Optional callback manager.
   212→            **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments (e.g. temperature).
   213→
   214→        Returns:
   215→            ChatResult containing the model's response.
   216→        """
   217→        endpoint = self._endpoint
   218→
   219→        full_prompt = _build_gemma_prompt(messages)
   220→
   221→        instance = {
   222→            "prompt": full_prompt,
   223→            "max_tokens": self.max_output_tokens,
   224→            "temperature": kwargs.get("temperature", 0.1),
   225→            "top_p": 0.95,
   226→            "top_k": 40,
   227→        }
   228→        parameters = {
   229→            "max_output_tokens": self.max_output_tokens,
   230→            "temperature": kwargs.get("temperature", 0.1),
   231→        }
   232→
   233→        start_time = time.time()
   234→        try:
   235→            logger.debug(
   236→                "Calling Vertex AI endpoint.predict for endpoint: %s",
   237→                self.endpoint_resource_name,
   238→            )
   239→            response = _predict_with_retry(
   240→                endpoint=endpoint, instances=[instance], parameters=parameters
   241→            )
   242→            duration = time.time() - start_time
   243→            logger.debug(
   244→                "Vertex AI endpoint.predict succeeded in %.2f seconds",
   245→                duration,
   246→            )
   247→        except Exception as e:
   248→            duration = time.time() - start_time
   249→            logger.warning(
   250→                "Vertex AI endpoint.predict failed after %.2f seconds: %s",
   251→                duration,
   252→                e,
   253→                exc_info=True,
   254→            )
   255→            raise
   256→
   257→        text = response.predictions[0]
   258→        text = _strip_model_garden_artifacts(text, full_prompt)
   259→
   260→        message = AIMessage(content=text)
   261→        generation = ChatGeneration(message=message)
   262→        return ChatResult(generations=[generation])
   263→
   264→    @property
   265→    def _llm_type(self) -> str:
   266→        return "vertex_model_garden"
   267→
   268→
   269→def _validate_vertex_config(cfg: AgentConfig) -> tuple[str, str, str, str]:
   270→    """Validate Vertex AI configuration fields.
   271→
   272→    Args:
   273→        cfg: AgentConfig instance.
   274→
   275→    Returns:
   276→        Tuple of (project_id, region, endpoint_id, vertex_model_name).
   277→
   278→    Raises:
   279→        ValueError: If required configuration is missing.
   280→    """
   281→    project_id = (cfg.gcp_project_id or "").strip()
   282→    region = (cfg.gcp_region or "").strip()
   283→    endpoint_id = (cfg.vertex_endpoint_id or "").strip()
   284→    vertex_model_name = (cfg.vertex_model_name or "").strip()
   285→
   286→    if not project_id:
   287→        raise ValueError("GCP_PROJECT_ID is required when MODEL_BACKEND=vertex")
   288→    if not region:
   289→        raise ValueError("GCP_REGION is required when MODEL_BACKEND=vertex")
   290→    if not endpoint_id and not vertex_model_name:
   291→        raise ValueError(
   292→            "VERTEX_ENDPOINT_ID or VERTEX_MODEL_NAME is required when "
   293→            "MODEL_BACKEND=vertex"
   294→        )
   295→
   296→    return project_id, region, endpoint_id, vertex_model_name
   297→

   299→class LocalMedGemmaChatModel(BaseChatModel):
   300→    """LangChain ChatModel wrapper for locally-loaded MedGemma.
   301→
   302→    Loads google/medgemma-4b-it (or a custom model path) via HuggingFace
   303→    ``transformers`` with optional bitsandbytes quantization. The model
   304→    and tokenizer are loaded once on first call and reused.
   305→
   306→    Requires:
   307→        - NVIDIA GPU with sufficient VRAM (8 GB+ for 4-bit, 16 GB+ for 8-bit)
   308→        - ``torch``, ``transformers``, ``accelerate`` packages
   309→        - ``bitsandbytes`` package (for quantized loading)
   310→    """
   311→
   312→    model_path: str = Field(default="google/medgemma-4b-it")
   313→    quantization: str = Field(default="4bit")
   314→    max_output_tokens: int = Field(default=4096)
   315→    _model: Any = None
   316→    _tokenizer: Any = None
   317→
   318→    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
   319→        """Validate that required packages are available."""
   320→        pass  # Defer actual loading to first call for fast startup
   321→
   322→    def _ensure_loaded(self) -> None:
   323→        """Load model and tokenizer on first use."""
   324→        if self._model is not None:
   325→            return
   326→
   327→        try:
   328→            import torch
   329→            from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
   330→        except ImportError as exc:
   331→            raise ImportError(
   332→                "Local MedGemma backend requires torch, transformers, and "
   333→                "accelerate installed. Install with: "
   334→                "pip install torch transformers accelerate bitsandbytes"
   335→            ) from exc
   336→
   337→        if not torch.cuda.is_available():
   338→            raise RuntimeError(
   339→                "Local MedGemma backend requires an NVIDIA GPU with CUDA. "
   340→                "No CUDA device detected. Use MODEL_BACKEND=vertex for "
   341→                "cloud-based inference instead."
   342→            )
   343→
   344→        logger.info(
   345→            "Loading MedGemma locally: model=%s, quantization=%s",
   346→            self.model_path,
   347→            self.quantization,
   348→        )
   349→
   350→        model_kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {
   351→            "device_map": "auto",
   352→            "torch_dtype": torch.bfloat16,
   353→        }
   354→
   355→        if self.quantization in ("4bit", "8bit"):
   356→            try:
   357→                from transformers import BitsAndBytesConfig
   358→            except ImportError as exc:
   359→                raise ImportError(
   360→                    "Quantized loading requires bitsandbytes. "
   361→                    "Install with: pip install bitsandbytes"
   362→                ) from exc
   363→
   364→            if self.quantization == "4bit":
   365→                model_kwargs["quantization_config"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(
   366→                    load_in_4bit=True,
   367→                    bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   368→                    bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
   369→                    bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4",
   370→                )
   371→            else:
   372→                model_kwargs["quantization_config"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(
   373→                    load_in_8bit=True,
   374→                )
   375→
   376→        start_time = time.time()
   377→        self._tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(self.model_path)
   378→        self._model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
   379→            self.model_path, **model_kwargs
   380→        )
   381→        duration = time.time() - start_time
   382→        logger.info("MedGemma loaded in %.1f seconds", duration)
   383→
   384→    def _generate(
   385→        self,
   386→        messages: list[BaseMessage],
   387→        stop: list[str] | None = None,
   388→        run_manager: CallbackManagerForLLMRun | None = None,
   389→        **kwargs: Any,
   390→    ) -> ChatResult:
   391→        """Generate a response using the locally-loaded MedGemma model.
   392→
   393→        Args:
   394→            messages: List of LangChain messages.
   395→            stop: Optional stop sequences.
   396→            run_manager: Optional callback manager.
   397→            **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments (e.g. temperature).
   398→
   399→        Returns:
   400→            ChatResult containing the model's response.
   401→        """
   402→        import torch
   403→
   404→        self._ensure_loaded()
   405→
   406→        full_prompt = _build_gemma_prompt(messages)
   407→
   408→        inputs = self._tokenizer(full_prompt, return_tensors="pt").to(
   409→            self._model.device
   410→        )
   411→        input_length = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
   412→
   413→        start_time = time.time()
   414→        with torch.no_grad():
   415→            outputs = self._model.generate(
   416→                **inputs,
   417→                max_new_tokens=self.max_output_tokens,
   418→                temperature=kwargs.get("temperature", 0.1),
   419→                top_p=0.95,
   420→                top_k=40,
   421→                do_sample=True,
   422→            )
   423→        duration = time.time() - start_time
   424→
   425→        # Decode only the generated tokens (exclude prompt)
   426→        generated_tokens = outputs[0][input_length:]
   427→        text = self._tokenizer.decode(generated_tokens, skip_special_tokens=True)
   428→        text = _strip_model_garden_artifacts(text, full_prompt)
   429→
   430→        logger.debug(
   431→            "Local MedGemma generated %d tokens in %.2f seconds",
   432→            len(generated_tokens),
   433→            duration,
   434→        )
   435→
   436→        message = AIMessage(content=text)
   437→        generation = ChatGeneration(message=message)
   438→        return ChatResult(generations=[generation])
   439→
   440→    @property
   441→    def _llm_type(self) -> str:
   442→        return "local_medgemma"
   443→
   444→
   445→def create_model_loader(config: AgentConfig | None = None) -> Callable[[], Any]:
   446→    """Create a lazy MedGemma model loader for the configured backend.
   447→
   448→    Supports two backends:
   449→        - ``vertex``: Vertex AI Model Garden (cloud, recommended for production)
   450→        - ``local``: Local GPU with HuggingFace transformers (requires NVIDIA GPU)
   451→
   452→    Args:
   453→        config: Agent configuration. Defaults to ``AgentConfig.from_env()``.
   454→
   455→    Returns:
   456→        Callable that loads and returns a LangChain chat model when invoked.
   457→
   458→    Raises:
   459→        ValueError: If required configuration is missing for the selected backend.
   460→    """
   461→    cfg = config or AgentConfig.from_env()
   462→
   463→    if cfg.backend == "vertex":
   464→        return _create_vertex_model_loader(cfg)
   465→
   466→    if cfg.backend == "ollama":
   467→        return _create_ollama_model_loader(cfg)
   468→
   469→    return _create_local_model_loader(cfg)
   470→
   471→
   472→def _create_vertex_model_loader(cfg: AgentConfig) -> Callable[[], Any]:
   473→    """Create a lazy Vertex AI model loader.
   474→
   475→    Args:
   476→        cfg: Validated AgentConfig with backend=="vertex".
   477→
   478→    Returns:
   479→        Callable that lazily initializes and returns the model.
   480→    """
   481→    project_id, region, endpoint_id, vertex_model_name = _validate_vertex_config(cfg)
   482→
   483→    @lazy_singleton
   484→    def load_model() -> Any:
   485→        try:
   486→            import vertexai
   487→        except ImportError as exc:
   488→            raise ImportError(
   489→                "Vertex AI backend requires google-cloud-aiplatform installed."
   490→            ) from exc
   491→
   492→        vertexai.init(project=project_id, location=region)
   493→
   494→        if vertex_model_name:
   495→            try:
   496→                from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
   497→            except ImportError as exc:
   498→                raise ImportError(
   499→                    "Vertex AI backend requires langchain-google-genai installed."
   500→                ) from exc
   501→
   502→            return ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
   503→                model=vertex_model_name,
   504→                project=project_id,
   505→                location=region,
   506→                vertexai=True,
   507→                max_output_tokens=cfg.max_new_tokens,
   508→            )
   509→
   510→        endpoint_resource_name = (
   511→            f"projects/{project_id}/locations/{region}/endpoints/{endpoint_id}"
   512→        )
   513→        return ModelGardenChatModel(
   514→            endpoint_resource_name=endpoint_resource_name,
   515→            project=project_id,
   516→            location=region,
   517→            max_output_tokens=cfg.max_new_tokens,
   518→        )
   519→
   520→    return load_model
   521→
   522→
   523→def _create_ollama_model_loader(cfg: AgentConfig) -> Callable[[], Any]:
   524→    """Create a lazy Ollama model loader via LangChain ChatOllama.
   525→
   526→    Args:
   527→        cfg: AgentConfig with backend=="ollama".
   528→
   529→    Returns:
   530→        Callable that lazily initializes and returns the model.
   531→    """
   532→
   533→    @lazy_singleton
   534→    def load_model() -> Any:
   535→        try:
   536→            from langchain_community.chat_models import ChatOllama
   537→        except ImportError as exc:
   538→            raise ImportError(
   539→                "Ollama backend requires langchain-community installed. "
   540→                "Install with: pip install langchain-community"
   541→            ) from exc
   542→
   543→        logger.info(
   544→            "Initializing Ollama model: base_url=%s, model=%s",
   545→            cfg.ollama_base_url,
   546→            cfg.ollama_model,
   547→        )
   548→        return ChatOllama(
   549→            base_url=cfg.ollama_base_url,
   550→            model=cfg.ollama_model,
   551→            temperature=0.1,
   552→        )
   553→
   554→    return load_model
   555→
   556→
   557→def _create_local_model_loader(cfg: AgentConfig) -> Callable[[], Any]:
   558→    """Create a lazy local MedGemma model loader.
   559→
   560→    Loads MedGemma (default: google/medgemma-4b-it) from HuggingFace
   561→    with optional 4-bit or 8-bit quantization via bitsandbytes.
   562→
   563→    Args:
   564→        cfg: AgentConfig with backend=="local".
   565→
   566→    Returns:
   567→        Callable that lazily initializes and returns the model.
   568→    """
   569→
   570→    @lazy_singleton
   571→    def load_model() -> Any:
   572→        logger.info(
   573→            "Initializing local MedGemma: model=%s, quantization=%s",
   574→            cfg.model_path,
   575→            cfg.quantization,
   576→        )
   577→        return LocalMedGemmaChatModel(
   578→            model_path=cfg.model_path,
   579→            quantization=cfg.quantization,
   580→            max_output_tokens=cfg.max_new_tokens,
   581→        )
   582→
   583→    return load_model
   584→
